# Bot Fleet v2 — Rules Catalog

**Generated:** 2026-08-12
**Files inventoried (project folder + archive, excluding `.git/`):** 326
**Files rules were extracted from (prose containing explicit rules):** 55 — `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md`, `STATUS.md`, all 50 Markdown files under `docs/`, and 2 Markdown files under `data/` (`data/archive/README-v1-ledger.md`, `data/receipts/README.md`)
**Archive folder in scope:** Yes. `data/archive/` (the frozen v1 ledger and its README) was inventoried and its prose read; per the project's own rule (`CLAUDE.md` §3 item 1), the archive's raw CSVs are cited as history only and are never a reporting input — that same rule is why this catalog does not extract "rules" out of `data/archive/*.csv` itself, only out of the accompanying README.
**Total rules extracted:** 2,310, grouped below under 56 source documents, ordered per the project's own file map (`CLAUDE.md` §6: root → cold-read core → operations → research references → history & logs → data/archive), with the ≈28 dated working/decision documents not named in that map placed last, ordered chronologically by the date embedded in their filename (undated ones alphabetically).

## Known gaps and scope notes

This audit was run by dispatching parallel sub-agents per document (or small document group), each independently reading its assigned file(s) in full and extracting matching rules; a synthesis pass then merged, grouped, and cross-referenced all outputs into this single table. The following limitations are flagged explicitly rather than silently smoothed over, per the task's own instruction to prefer "unclear" over a guess:

- **`docs/AI Agentic.pdf` (20MB) could not be read.** It is listed in the project's own file map (§6, research references) but the sub-agent assigned to it reported it does not exist at any searched path in the working copy used for this audit. No rules could be extracted from it. If it exists on the source machine, it should be re-supplied and this file re-audited.
- **Non-prose files were inventoried but intentionally not mined for rules**, per the task's own instruction to extract rules only from prose (or from prose describing how to use a data file): the ~183 non-README files under `data/` (CSVs, JSON receipts, capture `.txt`/`.tsv`/screenshot files — raw evidence, not narrative), the 25 files under `scripts/` (`.py`/`.sh` — code, explicitly "Claude Code's lane" per `CLAUDE.md` §7, not this catalog's), `.claude/settings.local.json` (tool config), `.env`/`.env.example`/`.gitignore`/`.DS_Store`/`dashboard.html` (non-documentation artifacts), and a `_to_delete/` folder (56 files — git-lock-troubleshooting debris from an earlier session, per this project's own device-bridge conventions, not project content). One additional prose-adjacent file, `data/captures/2026-08-03-pilot/FINISH-capture-diff-2026-08-04.md`, was inventoried but not individually read; it appears to be a capture-diff record already summarized within `docs/pilot-clone-card-qqq-fortress.md`.
- **Cross-document deduplication (process step 5) is best-effort, not exhaustive**, at this scale (2,310 rows across 56 documents). Where a source sub-agent explicitly flagged a row as a duplicate/restatement of another document's rule, that is preserved in the row's own Impacts column (e.g. "(duplicate)", "(restated)"). A small number of near-identical rules may still appear in two documents without a cross-reference; this catalog favors completeness (extracting a real rule twice) over the risk of silently dropping one.
- **The table below preserves each source sub-agent's own row exactly as extracted** (label, paraphrase, impacts, status, quote) rather than re-summarizing; this is deliberate, so every quote stays independently checkable against its source file without a second layer of paraphrase risk.

---

> ### ⛔ SCOPE AMENDMENT — 2026-08-17 · `R-2026-08-17-GIT-RULE-SCOPE`
>
> **Read before any row in this catalog that states the git prohibition.** Every such row is left
> standing verbatim, and every quote is left untouched — this banner scopes them, it does not
> rewrite them.
>
> The prohibition binds **bridge sessions operating on the mounted tree**. There it remains
> **total, including read-only git commands** — `.git/logs/HEAD` and loose-object reads answer
> every question git would ask, with no lock taken. It does **not** bind git inside an agent's own
> cloud container or clone: no mount, no unlink restriction, nothing to strand. That is how cloud
> agents open PRs.
>
> `CLAUDE.md` §9.1 step 3 — **Andy runs every commit to the main tree** — is **unchanged**.
>
> Rows scoped by this banner — line numbers **as they stand in this file after this banner was
> inserted**, re-verified 2026-08-17: **253** (git prohibition, restated), **1273**, **1488**,
> **1774** (§0.1 standing fact 1), **1793** (§0.3 gate A11).
> Row **122** (§9.1 step 3) is untouched by the amendment. Rows **2040** and **2046** are
> historical method statements about an audit that already ran — records, not standing rules.

Canary not run / stop never fires / count not recorded first → C10 stays OPEN; never infer the unit from `dstop`'s sign convention. | — | Active | "⛔ **do not re-derive `<D100>` yourself** — the rung basis is signed ruling R-1 and changing it is a plan amendment." |

### `docs/evidence-standards.md` (108 rules)

| Rule label | What the rule is | Impacts other areas | Status | Source quote/anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| §6.1 | When reporting counts of trades, always say "positions" or "condors" — never a bare, unlabeled count, since a leg-count and a position-count for the same trades differ. | STATUS.md, scripts/report.py output; CLAUDE.md §4 restates the same labelling law (inferred) | Active | "Say 'positions' or 'condors', never a bare count." — §6.1 |

| §0.1 | The platform has no persistence between scans except tags and SmartStops' high-water mark; no condition can reference its own past. Any mechanic described as "sustained"/"confirmed"/"after N minutes" must be built as a tag ladder or is an unrecorded substitution. | §5.3, §6.2, §11, hedge-research.md | Active | "No condition can reference its own past… is either a tag ladder nobody built, or a substitution nobody recorded." |
| §0.2 (evidence tiers) | Every claim must carry one of six evidence tags ([DOCUMENTED]/[FIRST-HAND]/[PROJECT-RULE]/[SINGLE-SOURCE]/[DOCS-SILENT]/[CONFLICT]); a doc-sourced claim is not automatically stronger than a first-hand screenshot. | All sections of this doc; evidence-standards.md | Active | "a doc-sourced claim is **not** automatically stronger than a screenshot of the running account." |
| §0.2 | Precedence rule: where a first-hand screenshot and a stale doc disagree, the screenshot wins; where memory and a doc disagree, the doc wins. | §0.2 provenance rule | Active | "Where a first-hand screenshot and a stale doc disagree, the screenshot wins. Where memory and a doc disagree, the doc wins." |
| §0.2 PROVENANCE RULE | A tier tag must name what was observed and when; citing another project document is not provenance (two docs vouching for each other is a citation loop); inference from absence ("I did not see a control") is never an observation. | oa-ops-runbook.md, all project docs citing each other | Active | "A tier tag must name what was observed and when. Citing another project document is NOT provenance… Inference from absence is not observation." |

| §6.2 | Touch is subject to the Bid-Ask Guard (disables Exit Options while the spread is wide); required design: leave the Bid-Ask Guard OFF on touch hedges and keep a shadow monitor as the divergence detector. | §6.3 | Active | "Guard: leave the Bid-Ask Guard OFF on touch hedges, and keep a shadow monitor as the divergence detector." |
| §6.2 | The original open question ("does Touch mean underlying-touches-strike or position-price-touches-a-level") is preserved for audit but resolved above. | §9 Check #1 | Superseded | "**Superseded — the original open question, preserved for audit:** … does Touch mean *the underlying touches the short strike*, or *the position price touches a level*?" |
| §6.3 | The Bid-Ask Guard, while ON, disables Exit Options from closing and pauses high%/low% tracking whenever the spread exceeds the configured width; currently OFF on the champion, HedgeD and the Fortress line. Decide per bot deliberately and record the decision. | build/clone specs | Active | "Turning it on is a silent-suppression generator. Decide per bot, deliberately, and record the decision." |
| §6.4 | Exit Options evaluate mid-price; a triggered order stays active exactly 2 minutes with no additional orders sent during that window, then cancels and re-checks. Both halves are now [DOCUMENTED]; no stacked-exit design may assume a second attempt inside the 2-minute window. | §8.2 attribution guard | Active | "Orders triggered by an exit option will remain active for two minutes; during that time, no additional orders will be sent to your broker." |
| §6.4 | The Expiration Exit Option is a documented exception to one-attempt-per-interval: if its order doesn't fill, the bot keeps sending orders for the rest of the trading day. Which trigger class a time exit uses must be recorded in the capture. | §8.2 | Active | "the bot will continue sending orders for the remainder of the trading day." |
| §6.5 | Whether re-applying "Update Position Exit Options" is side-effect-free is DOCS-SILENT; must be checked in the UI before any re-assertion watchdog is built on it. | §0.3 fix design | Gated — Pending | "Whether Update Position Exit Options can be re-applied repeatedly without generating spurious orders is **[DOCS-SILENT]**… Check it in the UI before building on it." |
| §7 | SmartPricing mode table (Fast/Normal/Patient/Off/Market, price counts, timings) is [DOCUMENTED + FIRST-HAND]; Fast's internal value is `speedy`, not "fast" — any parser keying on "fast" will silently miss it. | capture/config parsing | Active | "`speedy`** — any capture-diff or config parser keying on the string 'fast' will silently miss it." |
| §7 | The Final Price control's actual range is 50–150 (floor is mid, not bid); a better-than-mid final price is not settable. Resolves the §7 conflict and closes §9 Check #6. | §9 Check #6 | Active | "The range is 50–150… A final price better than the mid is **not settable**." |

## Research references


### `docs/backtest-ingest-protocol.md` (25 rules)

| Rule label | What the rule is | Impacts other areas | Status | Source quote/anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| unlabeled | Apply the oa-mirror-reference.md §3 evidence standards and the methodology.md factor-bucket lens throughout every backtest ingest; always compare by R, never raw P/L. | oa-mirror-reference.md §3; methodology.md | Active | "Apply the `oa-mirror-reference.md` §3 evidence standards and the `methodology.md` factor-bucket lens throughout. Compare by **R**, never raw P/L." — header |
| unlabeled | Every batch requires three artifacts — Compare-page screenshot, per-column Settings/Details screenshot, and positions.csv — none is redundant. | Step 1/2/3 procedure (below) | Active | "Three artifacts. All three are wanted; none is redundant." — What Andy pastes |
| unlabeled | If only some artifacts arrive, Claude proceeds with what's present and explicitly names what it could not verify. | Same section | Active | "Claude proceeds with what's present and **names what it could not verify**" — What Andy pastes |
| unlabeled | A discovery-phase finding must never skip straight to deployment — discovery and confirmation are governed by different rule sets and must not be conflated. | Discovery/confirmation gate structure (below) | Active | "never let a discovery finding skip to deployment." — Two speeds |
| DISCOVERY mode | In discovery, pre-registration and per-cell null testing are NOT required; screening may be broad, but four protections stay mandatory. | Rows below (OOS quarantine, Trial ledger, Exploratory tag, Compare-by-R) | Active | "Pre-registration NOT required. Per-cell null NOT required...The only four rules that stay on" — DISCOVERY mode |
| OOS quarantine (the firewall) | During discovery, explore in-sample only; never touch the OOS holdout (default 2024–26) — never "just peek." | Window policy §; Step 3 OOS procedure (restated there) | Active | "Explore **in-sample only**; do NOT touch the OOS holdout...Never 'just peek' at OOS." — DISCOVERY mode, rule 1 |
| Trial ledger | Tally every variant run in a discovery session — it's the only way to deflate a winner later (best-of-3 needs a small margin; best-of-40 a large one). | Step 4 verdict trial-count requirement (restated there) | Active | "Tally every variant run in the session...the only way to deflate a winner later" — DISCOVERY mode, rule 2 |
| Exploratory tag | Everything found in discovery is tagged "exploratory" — a hypothesis for a fresh test, never a verdict, regardless of how good it looks. | Confirmation-mode gate (below); Step 4 tagging | Active | "Everything found in discovery is tagged **exploratory**...NEVER a verdict" — DISCOVERY mode, rule 3 |
| CONFIRMATION mode | Confirmation survivors must be pre-registered, beat both the no-filter control and the random-day null, beat the best single component (for combos), and pass the OOS holdout before paper; only confirmation survivors earn capital. | Step 2/Step 3 gate checks (below) | Active | "beat the no-filter control AND the random-day null...then the OOS holdout; then paper. Only confirmation survivors earn capital." — CONFIRMATION mode |
| unlabeled | An exploratory winner is never deployed directly — it must be re-run as a pre-registered confirmation test on quarantined OOS data before it papers. | Discovery/Confirmation bridge | Active | "an exploratory winner is never deployed — it must be re-run as a pre-registered confirmation test" — "The rule that bridges them" |
| unlabeled | In-sample backtest window = the full available window including 2022; 2022 must not be excluded as a "bad year" since it's the best year/target regime for high-vol directional strategies. | Regime concentration check (Step 3, below) | Active | "2022 is NOT an outlier 'bad year' to remove...Cutting it deletes the signal, not noise." — Window policy |
| unlabeled | The new pre-2022 (2013+) history is only used for longer-dated/45-DTE directional variants, not for 0DTE (whose true window is only ~2022→now). | Directional pillar backtests (inferred) | Active | "The new 2013 history only helps for longer-dated / 45-DTE directional variants...use it there." — Window policy |
| unlabeled | Window choice (in-sample vs OOS split) is not a knob to tune for pretty numbers — in-sample is all of it, OOS is the pre-committed slice, and neither is shopped. | OOS quarantine rule (above) | Active | "Window choice is NOT a knob to tune for pretty numbers...don't shop." — Window policy |
| unlabeled | Step 1 requires confirming exactly one factor varies across compared columns (the pre-register rule); any off-spec setting must be flagged first and that column's result treated as suspect. | Step 2 headline grid (depends on this) | Active | "Confirm only one factor varies across columns (the pre-register rule). ...If a setting is off-spec, say so first" — Step 1 |
| unlabeled | Step 2's headline grid requires N≥100 and span≥6mo (flag any column failing), monotonic effect check, beat-control check (necessary but not sufficient), and treats win rate as context only, never the verdict. | Step 3 random-day null (necessary complement, below) | Active | "**N ≥ 100** and **span ≥ 6 mo**? Flag any column that fails...Win rate is context only — **never the verdict**." — Step 2 |
| unlabeled | For HEDGE batches, worst-loss/tail are first-class metrics judged against an "unstopped" control, with a tail count (trades worse than 3× avg credit); verdict is the (RoR, worst-loss) pair jointly. | Hedge tournament (strategy-taxonomy.md, inferred) | Active | "worst-loss and tail are first-class, not secondary...always against **unstopped** as control...the (RoR, worst-loss) pair *jointly*" — Step 2, HEDGE batches |
| IC FIRST STEP | For any IC tournament, group legs/spreads into condors by (day, entry window) before computing WR/R, or you'll read the leg-level ~48% WR trap. | Strategy-taxonomy.md IC pillar; hedge tournament data | Active | "pair the two spreads into one condor by (day, entry window) BEFORE computing WR/R, or you'll read the leg-level ~48% WR trap." — Step 3 |
| unlabeled | Random-day null pass bar: gated RoR must exceed control RoR AND null p<0.05 (the c1_analyze.py method); beating control alone is not a pass. | Step 2 beat-control check (complements it) | Active | "pass bar = gated RoR > control RoR **AND** null p < 0.05...Beating control alone is not a pass." — Step 3 |
| unlabeled | If one calendar-year regime (e.g. 2022) carries most of a batch's edge, an OOS holdout check is required. | OOS holdout procedure (below) | Active | "Flag if one regime (e.g. 2022) carries most of the edge ⇒ **OOS holdout required**." — Step 3 |
| unlabeled | The IS/OOS split is fixed at session start (default holdout 2024–26); sweeping happens in-sample only, OOS is touched only for the final 1-2 survivors, and the edge must survive in ≥2 distinct regimes. | Restates/elaborates the OOS quarantine rule and window policy above | Active | "the IS/OOS split is fixed at session start...Require the edge to survive in **≥2 distinct regimes**" — Step 3 |
| unlabeled | Step 4 verdict must state PASS/FAIL vs control+null/OOS, tag the read confirmatory or exploratory, and state the trial count with a deflation applied for it. | Trial ledger rule (above, restated) | Active | "Tag the read **confirmatory**...or **exploratory**...**State the trial count** and deflate the verdict for it." — Step 4 |
| unlabeled | Step 4 requires updating `current-state.md` (relevant pillar section) and appending `session-log.md` after every ingest. **Possible stale reference** — see conflicts section. | current-state.md (retired per history-index.md); session-log.md | Active (but references a file history-index.md says was retired) | "**Update `current-state.md`** (the relevant pillar §) and **append `session-log.md`**." — Step 4 |
| unlabeled | Step 4 must name the next batch in the queue, per `directional-oa-build-sheet.md`'s RUNNING QUEUE, which is authoritative for Directional. | directional-oa-build-sheet.md | Active | "the next batch in the queue (`directional-oa-build-sheet.md` RUNNING QUEUE is authoritative for Directional)." — Step 4 |
| unlabeled | Apply a ~30–40% haircut to OA backtest absolute economics (LEAN fills run ~4× hot); use R and relative rankings for the decision, with OA-paper as the actual gate. | Live-capital decision gate (inferred link to CLAUDE.md §4) | Active | "apply a ~30–40% haircut to absolute economics. Use R and relative rankings for the decision; OA-paper is the gate." — Notes/gotchas |
| unlabeled | The backtest CSV's Max Loss/Max Profit are structural bounds, not intraday excursion — touch/excursion-dependent hedge questions still require LEAN/Option Omega data. | Hedge tournament data sourcing (inferred) | Active | "the CSV does NOT contain the intraday P/L path — touch/excursion-dependent hedge questions still need LEAN/OO." — Notes/gotchas |

### `docs/directional-oa-build-sheet.md` (34 rules)

| Rule label | What the rule is | Impacts other areas | Status | Source quote/anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| unlabeled (header) | Every backtest column must be judged on Return-on-Risk, Profit Factor, and Worst Loss; win rate and total P/L are never acceptable judging criteria. VIX is the designated regime gate. | Applies to every batch in the doc; cites C1 as the regime-signal validator (inferred link to `hedge-research.md`/C1 research) | Active | "Judge on **Return-on-Risk + Profit Factor + Worst Loss**, NEVER win rate or total P/L." — header |
| unlabeled (header) | Testing must proceed one factor per batch, each measured against its own control, reading main effects rather than searching for the single best cell. | All subsequent batches (methodology discipline referenced repeatedly, e.g. Batch 4, Batch M) | Active | "Sequence = one factor per batch, each vs its control (methodology discipline… not the best cell)." — header |
| "The time-saver" section | OA's Compare Backtests page caps at 5 columns (A–E) per run. | Batch 6c/6d explicitly use "all 5 Compare columns" (inferred dependency on this cap) | Active | "OA's Compare page holds up to **5 columns (A–E)**." |
| "The time-saver" section | Variant columns must be built by duplicating the fully-built control column and changing exactly one field; hand-building B/C/D from scratch is prohibited. | Governs the build procedure for every batch in the doc | Active | "Never hand-build B/C/D from scratch — duplicate A so the shared settings are guaranteed identical." |
| Batch 1 | PASS for a regime gate = the gated columns beat the no-filter control on RoR, ideally with a monotonic improvement as the threshold tightens (A<B<C<D). | Reused in Batch 1b (threshold extension) and Batch 4 (gate discovery) | Active | "PASS = the VIX-gated columns beat Column A on RoR, ideally improving as the threshold rises." |
| Running Queue (intro) | Batches must pre-register a motivated hypothesis, beat control, and confirm OOS; sweeping all filter variants to find the best cell (the "95→57 trap") is prohibited. | Cited again in Batch 4 Stage 4a/4b and Batch M | Active | "Do NOT sweep all 8 Range filters for the best cell (the 95→57 trap)." |
| Batch 3 follow-up (repeated Batch 6c) | On a debit spread, stop-loss settings ≥100% are meaningless (loss is already capped at 100% of debit); only sub-100% rungs (e.g. 40/50/75/90) are meaningful test points. | Applies to both the put (Batch 3b) and call (Batch 6c) exit sweeps — restated once, noted here | Active | "SL≥100% = no stop; the meaningful rungs are sub-100% (50/75/90)." |
| Batch 3 RESULT (first pass) | Provisional paper-bot champion set to PT100/no-SL unless a sub-100% stop rung beats it on the tail. | Superseded same day by the Batch 3 clean re-run (SL75) | Superseded | "New champion config for the paper bot = **C/no-SL unless a sub-100% rung beats it on the tail.**" |
| Batch 3 clean re-run | Converged/locked put config = VIX≥22 · PT100 · SL75 · 11:00 entry; this explicitly replaces the no-SL config. | Feeds Batch 9 (entry sweep base), Batch 4 Pass 1/2 (benchmark), OOS holdout appendix; cites `hedge-research.md` as vindicated | Active | "NEW CONVERGED CONFIG: put · VIX≥22 · PT100 · SL75 · 11:00… **Supersedes the no-SL config.**" |
| Batch 4 (dual control) | A candidate regime gate is adopted only if it beats both the no-filter control and the VIX≥22 incumbent (or a combo beats VIX≥22 alone). | Governs Stage 4a/4b gate-discovery screening | Active | "Adopt only if it beats VIX≥22, or a combo beats VIX≥22-alone." |
| Batch 4 Stage 4a | Gap % is barred as already-falsified; GEX is barred per the FlashAlpha verdict; RSI/MACD/Stoch/CCI/Momentum oscillators are barred from the screen unless a specific written hypothesis is first produced. | References FlashAlpha verdict (external research finding) | Active | "**GEX stays OUT**… **skip the RSI/MACD/Stoch/CCI/Momentum oscillators**… unless one earns a specific written hypothesis first." |
| Batch 4 Stage 4b | Combinations may pair at most two survivors that measure different things; the combo must beat the best single component or it is overfitting; sweeping the full indicator cross-product is prohibited; at most one combo may graduate. | Governs Batch 4 Pass 3 (never triggered — see Pass 1/2 result) | Active | "Combine at most TWO survivors… the combo must ADD beyond the best single, or it's overfitting." |
| Batch 4 | Every gate survivor must be OOS-confirmed (2024–26) before it can inform bot design; a gate that only beats VIX in-sample is curve-fit. | Applies to the OOS-holdout appendix and Batch 6/6b call-gate validation | Active | "A gate that only beats VIX in-sample is curve-fit." |
| Batch 5 | Naked (single long option) structures are never sold; "naked" here means a defined-risk long option, not a short position. | Governs Batch 5 structure sweep and Family A/B structure choices | Active | "Naked = single long option, defined risk = premium. **Never sell naked.**" |
| Batch 4 Pass 1/2 RESULT | Regime gate is locked at VIX≥22 — no single or orthogonal-pair gate beat it; rally-day losses are ruled structural, not a fixable leak. | Used as the fixed benchmark in Batch 6/6b/6c (call track) and Batch M's cohort-gate boundary (VIX 15–22 band) | Active | "**GATE LOCKED = VIX≥22.**" |
| Batch 9 RESULT | Put entry time is locked at 11:00 ET; the put side is fully locked on every dimension (put · VIX≥22 · PT100 · SL75 · 11:00). | Feeds "After the backtests" graduation and mirrors into the call track's shared build settings | Active | "**ENTRY LOCKED = 11:00. PUT SIDE FULLY LOCKED ON EVERY DIMENSION**…" |
| Batch 7 RESULT | OR-break-down entry timing is disconfirmed as a hedge; fixed 11:00 entry is kept; Family A3 (down-break continuation) is disconfirmed as a consequence. | Explicitly disconfirms Family A3 in the structure-family section; (inferred) uses a "PT100/no-SL" baseline that predates the same-day SL75 lock (Batch 3 clean re-run) — an internal tension | Active | "The direction-confirmation lever (Family A3) is DISCONFIRMED… **KEEP fixed 11:00.**" |
| Batch 6 | The valid call-track test gate is the mirror of the put's (low absolute VIX AND VIX falling); testing calls on the put's VIX≥22 gate (as Batch 2 did) is not a fair test. | Reframes Batch 2's call result; governs Batch 6b/6c/6d sequencing | Active | "Batch 2 was NOT a fair call test… The fair call gate is the **MIRROR**." |
| Batch 6 (scaling note) | Testing VIX-change at multiple negative thresholds (−2/−3/−4) is a legitimate pre-registered test for the call structure, unlike the put side, where VIX-change is already falsified and further scanning would be a dredge. | Cross-references Batch 4 Pass 1 (put VIX-change falsification) | Active | "testing VIX-change at −2/−3/−4 scales IS motivated here… unlike the put side, where VIX-change is already falsified." |
| Batch 1b (recurring threshold) | Any regime-gate threshold must be checked against an approximately 100-trade sample floor; results below N≈100 are untrustable. | Recurs in Batch 6 (N=88 flagged), Batch 6b (adoption gate), and the OOS holdout appendix (N=76 flagged); (inferred) parallels CLAUDE.md §4's n≥100 evidence-tier rule | Active | "watch the **>100-trade floor**." |
| Batch 6b | A VIX-level cap on the call gate is adopted only if it beats the VIXΔ%<−2 incumbent on RoR AND clears N≥100. | Governs the go/no-go for the level-cap refinement | Active | "Adopt a cap only if it BEATS the VIXΔ-alone incumbent on RoR AND clears N≥100." |
| Batch 6b RESULT | Call gate is locked at VIX Change% < −2; the VIX-level cap is explicitly not adopted because it fails the random-day null. | Sets the fixed gate for Batch 6c's exit sweep | Active | "**GATE LOCKED = VIX Change% < −2.** The VIX-level cap is **NOT baked in.**" |
| Batch 6c/6d RESULT | Final locked call champion = Long Call Spread · VIX Change% < −2 · no PT (ride to settlement) · SL50 · 11:00. | Feeds the "automate→paper `DIR-SPX-CallVIXdrop`" graduation step; (inferred) requires pre-registration per CLAUDE.md §5 | Active | "**FINAL CALL CHAMPION (fully locked on every dimension, OOS-passed):** Long Call Spread · VIX Change% < −2 · NO PT · SL50 · 11:00." |
| Structure-family exploration (intro) | The three structure families (A/B/C) must not be run in parallel (dredge risk); all families must share the same VIX gate, no-filter control, random-day null, and OOS holdout. | Governs Family A/B/C sections directly below | Active | "Don't run all three families at once (dredge). All families share the same C1/VIX gate… random-day null + OOS holdout." |
| "THE FORK" (Batch 2 routing) | Batch 2's puts-vs-calls result determines which structure family is explored next: puts≫calls routes to Family A; calls also positive routes to Family B; Family C always runs as a parallel benchmark. | Directly tied to the Batch 2 result recorded earlier in the doc | Active | "**Puts ≫ calls** → explore **Family A**. **Calls also clearly positive** → explore **Family B**." |
| Family A2 | Pass criterion: the put ratio backspread must beat A1 on RoR AND improve Worst Loss/tail on deep-down days. | Compared against the Batch-1/9 locked put benchmark (A1) | Active | "**Pass:** beats A1 on RoR *and* improves Worst Loss/tail on the deep-down days." |
| Family A3 | Pass criterion: OR-break-down entry must beat fixed-11:00 A1 on RoR AND cut rally-day stop-outs. | Explicitly disconfirmed by the Batch 7 result | Superseded | "**Pass:** beats fixed-11:00 A1 on RoR *and* cuts the rally-day stop-outs." (disconfirmed: see Batch 7 RESULT) |
| Family B1 | Pass criterion: the long strangle/straddle must be positive, beat the put spread, and win on both up and down days (true direction-neutrality) to count as a pass. | Ties to the fleet's short-vol-heavy factor-bucket gap (inferred, `methodology.md`) | Active | "**Pass:** the long-vol structure is positive *and* beats the put spread *and* its wins come on BOTH up and down days." |
| Family C1 | Pass criterion: positive RoR with acceptable Worst Loss vs. unstopped; explicit caveat that even a pass does not diversify the fleet's factor mix since it remains short-vol. | Notes it "competes with the IC champion's lane" (inferred link to `build-plan.md` IC pillar) | Active | "even if it passes it's still short-vol… it monetizes the day but does **NOT** diversify the factor mix." |
| "Evaluation discipline (every batch)" | Every batch must be judged on Exp(R)/RoR + Worst Loss, must beat both control and a random-day sense-check, must be OOS-confirmed before papering, and must apply a ~30–40% backtest-to-live haircut plus real commission before any go-live math. | Applies fleet-wide across all batches/families; (inferred) parallels CLAUDE.md §4's evidence-tier haircut logic | Active | "Apply a ~30–40% backtest-to-live haircut + real commission before any go-live math." |
| Batch M | Shared cohort gate applies to all columns in the test; each track passes only if it beats the control on Exp(R)+Worst Loss AND beats the random-day null on the same cohort. | Cites `data/regime_coverage.md`; pairs with Batch 8 and "RESEARCH item 3" | Active | "Each track PASSES if it beats Col A on **Exp(R) + Worst Loss** AND beats the random-day null on the cohort." |

| unlabeled | Any quote-filter skip pattern must be checked for bias toward calmer days before trusting a gap or directional result. | directional-oa-build-sheet.md, c1_regime.py | Active | "**MUST verify this filter doesn't bias toward calmer days** before trusting any gap or directional result." |

| unlabeled | LEAN must be trusted only for win rate and relative rankings against the LEAN control baseline; LEAN's absolute RoR is never live economics — OA/paper validates absolute economics. | LEAN sweeps, OA paper validation | Active | "**trust LEAN for win rate + RELATIVE rankings only.**... **OA/paper validates absolute economics.**" |

### `docs/quantconnect-lean-exploration-brief.md` (18 rules)

| Rule label | What the rule is | Impacts other areas | Status | Source quote/anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| §2 step 1 | Research must pre-register 3–5 motivated hypotheses, never dredge, one factor at a time. | §8 overfitting guardrail (repeat); §10 | Active | "Pre-register hypotheses (3–5, motivated) — never dredge." — §2 |
| §2 step 3 | Results must be judged on R/Exp(R)+worst-loss, beat control AND a random-day null, then clear an OOS holdout. | §7 finding 2 | Active | "beat control AND a random-day null, then an OOS holdout." — §2 |
| §2 step 4 | LEAN survivors must be re-validated in OA for absolute economics before proceeding. | §5 decision rule (repeat); §7 finding 1 | Active | "Re-validate LEAN survivors in OA (absolute economics; OA = live engine)." — §2 |
| §2 step 5 | Candidates (especially hedges) must be paper-traded in OA before live — paper is mandatory. | §12b (repeat) | Active | "Paper in OA (proves fills — mandatory, especially for hedges)." — §2 |
| §2 step 6 | Graduation to live must be sized by F-001 (half-Kelly + 25% reserve) and respect F-004 (no martingale). | hedge-research.md §14 Martingale Gate (F-004) | Active | "sized by F-001 (half-Kelly + 25% reserve), F-004 (no martingale)." — §2 |
| §2 step 7 | Kill decisions must be made on data; trust the framework over the equity curve. | none noted | Active | "Kill on data (e.g., ORB 60m). Trust the framework over the equity curve." — §2 |
| §5 | OA is default+gate; reach for LEAN/code only for (a) hedges OA can't express, (b) sweeps too big to click, (c) null/OOS/classifier rigor; always re-validate LEAN in OA before paper. | §12a (repeat); §10 | Active | "Always re-validate LEAN survivors in OA before paper." — §5 |
| §6 (per-condor WR) | LEAN's built-in Win Rate is leg-level (~49% for any IC); agents must always compute per-condor win rate themselves. | §1 calibration numbers | Active | "Always compute per-condor win rate yourself." — §6 |
| §6 (fees) | Fees must be set per-contract via `set_security_initializer`; `portfolio.total_fees > 0` must be verified. | TradierSpxFeeModel; OA commission setting | Active | "Verify portfolio.total_fees > 0." — §6 |
| §6 (hedge granularity) | Hedges must be modeled at 1-minute reaction granularity, matching OA Monitors' poll rate. | hedge-research.md §12 (S2 5-min approx) | Active | "finer flatters the hedge." — §6 |
| §6 (calibration) | Every new LEAN sweep must be calibrated against a known OA number before being trusted. | §1 Phase 0 probe; §7 finding 1 | Active | "Always calibrate to a known OA number before trusting a new sweep." — §6 |
| §7 finding 1 | LEAN runs ~4× hot vs OA; trust LEAN only for win rate/relative ranking, never absolute economics. | §5 (repeat); §12a (repeat) | Active | "LEAN for win rate + relative ranking; OA/paper for absolute economics." — §7 |
| §7 finding 2 | LEAN's quote filter skips ~24% of days; must verify no bias toward calm days before trusting gap/directional results. | lean/c1_analyze.py bias check | Active | "Must verify it isn't biased toward calm days before trusting gap/directional reads." — §7 |
| §8 | Overfitting is the dominant autonomy risk; build the disciplined pre-registered runner, never a "perfect combination" dredge. | §2 step 1 (repeat); §10; §12c (repeat) | Active | "Build the disciplined version, never the dredge." — §8 |
| §8 (sandbox constraint) | The LEAN CLI/runner cannot run in Claude's sandbox; it must live on Andy's Mac or the VPS, with Claude reading results only from the mounted folder. | §12c (repeat) | Active | "Claude's sandbox cannot host the CLI... The runner runs on Andy's Mac or the VPS." — §8 |
| §10 | Six standing decisions not to be relitigated: engine=LEAN→OA→paper (OO dropped); calibration locked; C1=day-type labeler, GEX excluded; directional/paper in OA now, C1 in LEAN, code path deferred to hedge tournament; no brute-force dredge agent; §12a is the standing tooling reference. | §5, §6, §8, §12a-c | Active | "Don't build a brute-force 'perfect combination' agent — build the pre-registered disciplined runner." — §10 |
| §12c (hard gate) | LEAN CLI/API automation requires a paid QC org (Quant Researcher ≈$84/mo); free tier is paste-loop only. | §9 cost summary; §3 to-do item 19 | Active | "Hard gate: the CLI/API need a paid QC org." — §12c |
| §12c (upgrade timing) | The paid-tier upgrade is deferred until the hedge tournament begins; the directional paper bot ships on free tier. | §4 timeline | Active | "make the Researcher jump when the hedge tournament (Workstream A3/A5) starts." — §12c |

### `docs/strategy-taxonomy.md` (16 rules)

| Rule label | What the rule is | Impacts other areas | Status | Source quote/anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| unlabeled | Every bot is classified as "Pillar · Role" — two independent properties (strategy program + lifecycle job); underlying (SPX/QQQ/SPY) is a secondary attribute, not a pillar. | The four-Pillar model; OA grouping convention below | Active | "A bot is always '**Pillar · Role**', e.g. 'Iron Condor · Live-candidate'." — Core idea section |
| unlabeled | A morning go/no-go router classifies each session and routes it: chop days → Iron Condor, high-vol days → Directional. | Pillar 1 (IC) and Pillar 2 (Directional) daily operation | Active | "A **morning go/no-go router** classifies the session and routes it: **chop → Iron Condor**, **high-vol → Directional**." — Four Pillars, "The daily logic" |
| unlabeled | Two experiments (aggressive debit-buy vs. high-vol IC variant) run head-to-head for the Directional high-vol slot, decided by live data. | Pillar 2 build priority; backlog.md (inferred) | Active | "Two experiments run head-to-head for the high-vol slot, decided by live data" — Pillar 2 — Directional |
| unlabeled | OA-Mirror bots stay in the Mirror pillar permanently — they do not graduate out even after funding. | Pillar 3 funding workflow | Active | "Bots stay here (they do NOT graduate out)." — Pillar 3 — OA-Mirror |
| Graduation bar (paper → funded) | A Mirror bot is funded only once it reaches ≥20 trades AND positive P/L AND win-rate within ~10% of the source's claim AND max drawdown under the risk cap. | Mirror funding-watch tracking (roster table); backlog.md | Active | "≥20 trades **and** positive P/L **and** win-rate within ~10% of the source's claim **and** max drawdown under the risk cap." — Pillar 3 |
| unlabeled | Lab/R&D is distinct from the "Experiment" role: Experiment is a variant of a defined pillar strategy; Lab is a strategy with no pillar yet. | Pillar/Role taxonomy definitions | Active | "the role is a *variant of a defined pillar strategy*...Lab is a *strategy with no pillar yet*." — Pillar 4 |
| unlabeled | Graduation between roles is a role change, not the creation of a new bot. | Roles table; graduation workflow (repeated near "Core idea" section) | Active | "Graduation is a **role change, not a new bot**." — The Roles section |
| unlabeled | The "Mirror-watch" role applies to Pillar 3 (OA-Mirror) only. | Roles table | Active | "**Pillar 3 only.**" — The Roles table, Mirror-watch row |
| unlabeled | On the OA platform, Bot Group must equal Pillar — the four groups are IC, Directional, OA-Mirror, Lab. | OA bot organization; naming convention below | Active | "**OA Bot Group = Pillar** — the four groups are `IC`, `Directional`, `OA-Mirror`, `Lab`." — Naming & grouping convention |
| unlabeled | OA Tag must equal Role; because role is a tag, graduation is a retag, not a rename, so bot names stay stable for the bot's whole life. | OA bot organization | Active | "graduation is a retag, not a rename — names stay stable for a bot's whole life." — Naming & grouping convention |
| unlabeled | Bot names must follow the schema `Pillar-Underlying-Config-Hedge`, with defined codes for each slot (e.g. hedge codes S2/S3/SL100/Defang/Unstopped). | Every future bot build/clone (inferred link to oa-ops-runbook.md) | Active | "**Bot name = `Pillar-Underlying-Config-Hedge`**" — Naming & grouping convention |

| unlabeled | The hedge-selection tournament runs backtest-first (on the champion's clean SPX history, sidestepping contaminated QQQ hedge data) to narrow the field, then lives the 1-2 survivors as parallel copies of the champion. | QQQ hedge-family reclassification (below) | Active | "1. **Backtest** the bracket on the champion's *clean SPX history*...2. **Live the 1-2 survivors**" — Hedging tournament |
| unlabeled | The QQQ "Hedge family" is not a hedge tier — reclassified as five "Iron Condor · Experiment" bots, superseded by the backtest-first tournament approach. | Roster reclassification (roster table) | Superseded | "Reclassified to 'Iron Condor · Experiment'; superseded by the backtest tournament." — Hedging, "Consequence" |

### `docs/cross-functional-reference.md` (20 rules)

| Rule label | What the rule is | Impacts other areas | Status | Source quote/anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-001 | Never size a premium-selling strategy at full backtest-implied Kelly; run ~half-Kelly and hold ~25% margin in reserve for vol spikes. Applies to the aggregate fleet, sized at half-Kelly with reserve held at portfolio level (not per-bot). | Fleet allocation defaults (inferred: build-plan.md sizing rules) | Active | "Never size a premium-selling strategy at full backtest-implied Kelly. Run ~half-Kelly and hold ~25% margin in reserve" — §1 F-001 |
| F-004 | Any strategy/bot that recovers losses by sizing up the next entry (martingale) is a hard reject-on-sight, added to every project's kill-criteria; screen every bot's configurable inputs for this pattern. | Kill-criteria log (§6); every bot's config screen | Active | "Any strategy or bot that recovers losses by sizing up the next entry is high tail-risk. Reject on sight." — §1 F-004 |
| F-002 | Gate entry/activation on VIX-futures term-structure shape, not IVR alone: front-end backwardation → sell short-DTE premium aggressively; flat/elevated → widen strikes, reduce size; whole curve shifting up → sit out or go long vol. | Bot activation overlay (Iron Condor pillar, inferred) | Active | "Front-end backwardation...sell short-DTE premium aggressively...Whole curve shifting up → sit out, or go long vol." — §2 F-002 |
| F-005 | Enter double calendars only when VIX-futures front-end is in backwardation; do not enter when the curve is flat or in contango. | F-003 double calendar deployment gate (below) | Active | "Enter double calendars only when VIX-futures front-end is in backwardation...Do not enter when the curve is flat or in contango." — §2 F-005 |
| F-003 | Double calendar is only viable as a deployable structure with both the F-005 backwardation gate and automated roll logic at ~3 DTE; without both, do not deploy. | F-005 (above); Lab/R&D candidate structures (inferred) | Active | "only viable with (a) the F-005 backwardation gate and (b) automated roll logic at 3 DTE. Without both, do not deploy." — §3 F-003 |
| The triage schema | Apply the same 5-step schema to every bot identically: Classification, Risk flags, Mechanical breakdown, Gaps, Recommended next actions. | Bot-selection/onboarding methodology | Active | "1. Classification...2. Risk flags...3. Mechanical breakdown...4. Gaps...5. Recommended next actions." — §4 |
| unlabeled | Configurable-input count of 7+ is treated as a complexity/overfitting flag on a candidate bot. | Restated in the "Quick-reference checklist" (§ end) | Active | "7+ configurable inputs (F-014) materially complicates judging whether the edge is real or curve-fit." — §4 OA platform signals |
| unlabeled | No backtest is a flag, not an automatic reject — it shifts the burden to scout the author's channels for undisclosed backtests/trade logs before any verdict. | Restated in Quick-reference checklist | Active | "No backtest = flag, not an automatic reject, but it shifts the burden to 'scout the author's channels...' before any verdict." — §4 |
| unlabeled | A low clone-activation rate is treated as a flag signal (crowd cloned, then declined to deploy). | Bot-selection methodology | Active | "A low activation rate...is a flag — the crowd cloned and then declined to deploy" — §4 OA platform signals |
| unlabeled | Template age is a survivorship signal — an old template still live is weighed positively. | Bot-selection methodology | Active | "A 5-year-old template that's still live has survived multiple regimes — a positive, weighable signal" — §4 |
| Confidence ladder | Findings are rated High (multi-source + math/empirics), Medium (single strong source + plausible mechanism with pushback), Low (single unverified source with inconsistency), or Unverified (no corroboration). | Every F-NNN finding's confidence label | Active | "High — multi-source corroboration *plus* math/empirics support...Unverified — posted but no corroboration; flag for follow-up or skip." — §5 |
| unlabeled | A P&L brag with no disclosed mechanic, an author who won't disclose the rule, something already in the substrate, or unsupported opinion does NOT count as a finding. | Confidence ladder / substrate intake process | Active | "A single P&L brag with no disclosed mechanic...Opinion without supporting structure." — §5 What is NOT a finding |
| The governing heuristic | Cross-corroboration beats score — a lightly-upvoted post backed by a credible practitioner outranks a heavily-upvoted post with no informed responses. | Source-vetting throughout §7 | Active | "Cross-corroboration beats score. A 14-point post backed by a 30-year practitioner is higher confidence than a 1000-point post" — §5 |
| unlabeled | Never store a war-story/anecdote as the rule — extract only the underlying principle. | F-004's Captain Condor example (explicit in-doc link) | Active | "'X lost $50M' is the anecdote; the avoid-criterion is the finding. Never store the war story as the rule." — §6 |
| unlabeled | Never invent stats — any win rate/Sharpe/CAGR/follower count must be explicitly attributed to its source; unattributed numbers are not allowed. | Source citation discipline (inferred link to CLAUDE.md §4 evidence law) | Active | "Unattributed numbers are not allowed." — §6 |
| unlabeled | Tag conservatively — when unsure whether a finding applies to a bot, leave it untagged. | Finding-tagging process | Active | "When unsure whether a finding applies to a bot, leave it off." — §6 |
| unlabeled | Kill-criteria are cumulative — a bot can pass on structure and still die on a single hard criterion (e.g., F-004 or missing F-002 filter). | F-002, F-004 (above) | Active | "A bot can pass on structure and still die on a single hard criterion." — §6 |
| Kill-log | Skip any strategy whose author won't disclose the actual mechanic — opacity is the tell. | Strategy-discovery source vetting (§7) | Active | "'Won't disclose the mechanic' → skip...Pattern: opacity is the tell." — §7 Kill-log |
| Kill-log | Skip a strategy backed by a small sample plus an internal logical inconsistency between its stated thesis and its actual setup. | Strategy-discovery source vetting (§7) | Active | "Small sample + internal inconsistency → skip...a result that contradicts its own stated mechanism is noise." — §7 |
| Kill-log | Skip Reddit results filtered to `t=week`; use `t=year`/`t=all` instead, since weekly tops are dominated by brags/news. | Strategy-discovery sourcing cadence (§7 table) | Active | "`t=week` filters on any subreddit → skip...Use `t=year` / `t=all`" — §7 |

| Phase 2 Exit | Phase 2 exits only once a ruling auto-propagates to all docs with a verification report and gradings correctly refuse below threshold. | Propagator; Statistician gates | Active | "a ruling propagates to all docs automatically with a verification report; gradings correctly refuse below threshold" — §8 Phase 2 |
| Phase 3 Entry | Phase 3 (durability off-ramp) is entered only if a named dissent failure signal fires (resume-impossible gap, a missed hook gate, or scale outgrowing one session). | Kill-criteria §10 (the "escalation not abandonment" rule) | Active | "one of the dissent's failure signals fires (mid-run resume impossible with audit gap; hooks miss a gate; scale grows past a single session)" — §8 Phase 3 |
| Kill criteria | Any architectural (not one-line-bug) un-gated write that reaches Option Alpha even once requires immediate reversion to the hardened single session. | Gate-leakage risk register | Active | "Any un-gated state change reaches Option Alpha (not a doc) even once and the cause is architectural...revert immediately" — §10 |
| Kill criteria | If, after Phase 2, the audit trail cannot reproduce a graded conclusion end-to-end, revert — this violates the "non-reproducible runs are a defect" rule. | Reproducibility discipline | Active | "the audit trail cannot reproduce a graded conclusion end-to-end → the system violates your 'non-reproducible runs are a defect' rule; revert" — §10 |
| Kill criteria | If maintenance consumes more of the week than the research it supports, for two consecutive weeks, revert to Phase 0/1. | Solo-maintainability constraint | Active | "Maintenance consumes more of your week than the research it supports for two consecutive weeks → ...revert" — §10 |
| Kill criteria | If token/infra cost exceeds the value of time saved (example given: $400+/month to save an hour a day), revert to the cheaper design. | Cost budget (§1 estimate) | Active | "Token/infra cost exceeds the value of time saved...→ revert to the cheaper single-session design" — §10 |
| Kill criteria | Durable-execution failures (mid-run resume gaps) trigger escalation to Phase 3, not project abandonment. | Phase 3 entry (above) | Active | "Escalation, not abandonment, is the correct response to durable-execution failures...move to Phase 3 (LangGraph), don't kill the project." — §10 |
| The overriding rule | Because everything is git-tracked and Phase 0 stays runnable at every later phase, rollback is always cheap — this is what makes an aggressive migration safe for a solo operator. | All phases (§8) | Active | "because everything is git-tracked and Phase 0 remains runnable at every later phase, rollback is always cheap" — §10 |

| unlabeled | Sign-convention rule: use `openPrice` for magnitude or `abs(premium)` if `premium` must be used; never take `premium` at face value as "the credit." | build_ledger.py, execution_audit.py | Active | "Use `openPrice` when you want a magnitude. | unlabeled | `build_ledger.py`'s `credit` column is populated from `openPrice`; `premium` is a raw passthrough that keeps its sign — the two must not be confused. | trades.csv schema | Active | "the ledger's **`credit` column is populated from `openPrice`**... do not confuse the two." |
| unlabeled | The project's R convention (CLAUDE.md §4) uses the `ror` basis, not `returnPct`. | CLAUDE.md §4 | Active | "the project's R convention (`CLAUDE.md` §4) is the **`ror` basis**, not `returnPct`." |
| unlabeled | Time-exit counterfactuals cannot be decided from MFE/MAE data; they require a live A/B arm. | research-loop-spec.md §2 | Active | "Time-exit questions require a live A/B arm." |

### `docs/oa-reconciliation-report.md` (18 rules)

| Rule label | What the rule is | Impacts other areas | Status | Source quote/anchor |

| §0 Method and provenance | Defines the report's three verdict tiers: CONTRADICTED = a DOCUMENTED fact opposes the claim; UNSOURCED = no corpus fact speaks to it; CONFIRMED = a DOCUMENTED fact supports the claim as stated. | Governs every finding (R-01 through R-22) in the report | Active | "**CONTRADICTED** = a DOCUMENTED fact opposes the claim… **UNSOURCED**… **CONFIRMED** = a DOCUMENTED fact supports the claim." |
| §0 Method and provenance | Within a section, claims in step-by-step-executed docs (pilot card, reactivation runbook §§2–3, ops runbook) outrank claims in merely-read docs (platform reference, hedge research, mirror reference). | Governs ordering of R-01 through R-09 (CONTRADICTED section) | Active | "claims in **step-by-step-executed docs**… rank above claims in **merely-read docs**." |
| §0 Method and provenance | A DOCS-SILENT row counts as silence: a plausible project assumption does not pass as CONFIRMED merely because nothing in the corpus contradicts it. | Underlies R-15, R-16, R-17, R-19 findings | Active | "a plausible project assumption does not pass as CONFIRMED merely because nothing contradicts it." |
| §0 Method and provenance | The unresolved Wave-1 fingerprint mismatch on two pages is informational, not blocking, since every sampled quote from both is still an exact substring of a fresh fetch. | Affects citation validity of `data-feeds` and `autotrading-best-practices` facts used elsewhere | Active | "is **informational, not blocking** — every sampled quote from both pages is still an exact substring of a fresh fetch." |

| §0 | Firewall principle: generation (proposing) is free and daily; commitment (acting) is rare and gated. The loop may propose anything, change nothing. | CLAUDE.md §5; §7 | Active | "generation is free and daily; commitment is rare and gated." — §0 |

NEVER_REACHED, or UNDECIDABLE." — §2 |

| §3 | The variant set is declared once and changed only by a new signature; choosing variants after seeing the day is prohibited. | §10 (frozen 12) | Active — Frozen | "Choosing variants after seeing the day is storytelling, not experiment." — §3 |

| §3 (conditional rungs) | Conditional (trough-timing) rungs: stop at 100% if trough before 13:00; 100% if before 14:00; 200% if before 14:00. | §1a (v1 motivation) | Active | "stop at 100% if the trough came before 13:00... 200% if the trough came before 14:00." — §3 |
| §3 (time exits excluded) | Time exits are not a Track A variant; funded instead via Track B `Expiration` 0.015 vs 0.01 incumbent — the 0.005 (15:55) rung is unreachable because a 15:52 backstop always closes first. | §10 Track B allocation; track-b-arms-spec.md §6.4, §2, §11 item 10; oa-platform-reference.md §4.1 | Active (annotated 2026-08-05) | "R-2's time question is served by Expiration 0.015 (15:45) alone." — §3, 2026-08-05 annotation |
| §4 | If a question is worth asking twice, spend a Track B slot rather than rely on Track A. | §10 (Track B ≤8 slots) | Active | "Rule: if a question is worth asking twice, spend a slot." — §4 |
| §5 | Graduation gate: n≥100 closed positions per (bot,variant) pair AND ≥6 months AND spans a regime change AND beats control per the §10a permutation test with a pre-declared margin. | CLAUDE.md §4; §10a; pre-registration-ledger.md | Active (amended 2026-08-05; regime-change conjunct explicitly undefined) | "The gate cannot fire until it is written and signed." — §5 |

| §10a item 4 | Nightly monitoring of the gate statistic must use an always-valid confidence sequence, never a fixed-n CI. | §10a item 2 | Active | "not a fixed-n CI." — §10a item 4 |

---

## Conflicts

Contradictory rule pairs surfaced during extraction (per process step 6). Each entry cites both sides by document + label. Entries marked *(resolved in-doc)* are self-corrections already settled within one document — kept here because a reader consulting only one section could still be misled.

1. **Gate-label collision: two unrelated "G" systems share a prefix.** `docs/decision-memo-2026-08-04.md` D-1 defines gates **G1–G4** (bundle Bot Input mechanics: empty-value expressibility, capture legibility, Preset compatibility, propagation). `docs/comparative-machinery-spec.md` §8 independently defines gates **G-1 through G-16** (statistical-machinery rulings, e.g. G-1 = authorize `data/exit_rows.csv`). A citation of "G1" or "G-1" without naming the source file is ambiguous. — `decision-memo-2026-08-04.md` D-1 vs. `comparative-machinery-spec.md` §8.

2. **Market-pricing "one exception" vs. the `itmlive`/`itmpaper` = `market` ruling.** `docs/decision-memo-2026-08-04.md` Decision 5's amended rule bans Market pricing on every entry/exit "with one exception — a hard end-of-day flat close." D-3 in the same document separately rules `itmlive`/`itmpaper` = `market`, also a Market-priced closing mechanism (for ITM-expiring positions), distinct from the flat-close backstop and never explicitly reconciled as a second exception. — `decision-memo-2026-08-04.md` Decision 5 vs. D-3.

3. **PR-19 is structurally unwinnable under the currently-ruled gates.** `docs/comparative-machinery-spec.md` G-5 (unruled) leaves conjunct (c) applied to PR-19, whose own hypothesis is that it never fires; the gate's own text states "PR-19 can never pass GATE-CM" unless ruled otherwise — an acknowledged, still-open contradiction with the project's general fireable-kill-criterion design. — `comparative-machinery-spec.md` §8 G-5 vs. F-5 (A-14)/§7 CM-8.

4. **OA-Mirror pillar status: funded vs. watch-only.** `docs/oa-mirror-reference.md` §2.6 states "OA-Mirror is a funded pillar; bots fund IN PLACE" with a live funding bar (≥20 trades, positive P/L, WR within ~10%, drawdown cap). `CLAUDE.md` §1 labels the current pillar "OA-Mirror (**watch-only**)." These directly disagree on whether OA-Mirror bots are eligible for live capital. — `oa-mirror-reference.md` §2.6 vs. `CLAUDE.md` §1.

5. **Bot-Group "reconcile exactly" invariant undermined two paragraphs later.** `docs/oa-ops-runbook.md` §3 states Bot Group is single-select and "must reconcile to `bots_meta.csv`'s pillar column, **exactly**," then its own 2026-08-07 append records the account also carries an `IC-Focus` group alongside pillar group `IC` and rules "Not amended — both groups stay." — `oa-ops-runbook.md` §3 (base rule) vs. §3 (2026-08-07 append).

6. **Shared vs. forked tournament-arm automations — resolved only for one cohort.** `docs/oa-ops-runbook.md` §3's fork-via-Copy instruction (arms must NOT share a Library automation) is described in-text as conflicting with `docs/build-plan.md` §2D and `docs/hedge-research.md` §5.2, which require shared automation/inputs. The 2026-08-06 "Architecture E" ruling resolves this only for the greenfield family; the doc states the fork-via-Copy instruction "stands unchanged for every other cohort, where designed sharing has not been ruled." Also independently flagged, with the same underlying tension, in `docs/session-log.md` (2026-08-03, "DOC CONFLICT on the tournament build," resolved operationally by Decision 4 on 2026-08-04, but `oa-ops-runbook.md` §3's own wording was left "flagged, not amended"). — `oa-ops-runbook.md` §3 vs. `build-plan.md` §2D / `hedge-research.md` §5.2; also `session-log.md` (2026-08-03 finding 3).

7. **Session-continuity model tension** *(lower-confidence; may be a superseded older-project convention rather than a live conflict)*. `docs/oa-mirror-reference.md` §9 states "living docs regenerated at session end, old versions deleted," in tension with `CLAUDE.md` §9.1's append-only, git-tracked, "uncommitted work at session end is unfinished work" model. Nothing in either file states §9's rule has been retired. — `oa-mirror-reference.md` §9 vs. `CLAUDE.md` §9.1.

8. **T3 evidence gate: "does not exist" vs. "CLOSED... defined."** `docs/evidence-standards.md` §9.1 states "The T3 gate does not exist... T3 evidence currently has no home," while §10 item 2 in the same document lists the same item as "CLOSED 2026-07-31: defined (§4.5)." §9 is explicitly framed as a list of things that "do not add up," so this may be a known, unresolved internal seam rather than an oversight — but as written the two claims conflict. — `evidence-standards.md` §9.1 vs. §10 item 2 / §4.5.

9. **Possible duplicate `<FILL>`-blank items, both tagged audit H3.** `docs/evidence-standards.md` §8 names "the `<FILL>` blanks in `rules-of-engagement.md`" as "an active H3 failure" tied to the per-bot max-loss pre-registration field; §10 item 4 separately names "the four [`<FILL>` blanks] in `rules-of-engagement.md` (audit H3)" alongside the RoE $ cap (board G4). Unclear whether these describe the same blanks twice or genuinely distinct open items. — `evidence-standards.md` §8 vs. §10 item 4.

10. **Mirror universe size: 7 mirrors / 9 rows vs. 10 mirrors / 174 positions.** `CLAUDE.md` §3 item 1's main-text sentence says the mirror baseline snapshot covers "the 7 live mirrors," but its own bracketed correction on the same line says "WRITTEN 2026-08-04; 174 positions, **10 mirrors**, zero excluded" — an internal contradiction, and "not yet written"/"written" coexisting in the same sentence. `docs/build-plan.md` §2 Group C and §3 both say **7** mirrors / **nine rows**; `docs/daily-loop-spec.md` §3 also says "a one-time frozen snapshot, **nine rows**." Whether the mirror universe is 7/9 or 10/174 is unresolved across these sources. Related: `docs/pre-registration-ledger.md` §8 item 9 independently flags the same nine-vs-ten-row discrepancy and states it is "flagged here, not edited." — `CLAUDE.md` §3 item 1 vs. `build-plan.md` §2/§3 vs. `daily-loop-spec.md` §3 vs. `pre-registration-ledger.md` §8 item 9.

11. **Clones share automations "by reference" — stated as fact, then voided in the same document** *(resolved in-doc, flagged as a skim trap)*. `docs/pilot-clone-card-qqq-fortress.md` STEP 2 states as fact "Clones share automations BY REFERENCE... Edit the clone's automation and you have edited the original too," while a later banner in the same file states "VOID 2026-08-05 — THIS STEP'S PREMISE IS FALSE... Clones do NOT share automations by reference." The superseding rule lives in `docs/oa-ops-runbook.md` §5 Trap 1 (originally Trap 1 in the earlier r-edit-authorization pass; also self-corrected there — the original "clones share by reference" claim was struck 2026-08-05). — `pilot-clone-card-qqq-fortress.md` STEP 2 (original text) vs. its own VOID banner vs. `oa-ops-runbook.md` Trap 1.

12. **`LEDGER_START` definition: generic vs. a fixed date, and two competing rulings.** `docs/build-plan.md` §1 defines `LEDGER_START` generically as "the Day-0 reactivation date," while its own §3 amendment banner fixes it to a specific, non-moving date: "`LEDGER_START` = `2026-08-10`... FIXED: it does not move if switch-on slips." `CLAUDE.md` §3 item 1 still uses only the generic phrasing without citing the 2026-08-10 fix. Separately, this fixed-date ruling itself explicitly supersedes an earlier ruling: `docs/decision-card-2026-08-08.md` Slot 1 states it "SUPERSEDES a prior Andy ruling, on its face" — `docs/state.md`'s S0a/S0b (2026-08-07) text "the day you switch the first bot on IS `LEDGER_START`" — because the weekend switch-on case the earlier ruling didn't anticipate would otherwise have set it to 2026-08-08/09. (Session log carries both sides: `docs/session-log.md` ~L5964-5967/L6346-6353 for the superseded S0a ruling, ~L7443-7449 for the superseding fixed-date ruling.) — `build-plan.md` §1 vs. §3 amendment vs. `CLAUDE.md` §3 item 1; and `decision-card-2026-08-08.md` Slot 1 vs. `docs/state.md` S0a/S0b.

13. **Claude-in-Chrome / OA drivability contradiction.** `docs/oo-trial-backtests.md` header states "Claude-in-Chrome can drive OO (**OA is blocklisted**)," implying Claude-in-Chrome cannot touch OA — directly contradicting `CLAUDE.md` §5's OA automation authority ("Claude executes OA edits directly (Chrome-direct: read, drive, and save, in-session)"). Either the blocklist statement predates the 2026-08-04 authority amendment, or it refers to a different mechanism/account. — `oo-trial-backtests.md` header vs. `CLAUDE.md` §5.

14. **`ic-trailing-stop-backtest.md` cites the very source its own banner disowns.** The document's 2026-07-30 banner declares that sourcing constants from `data/bots_config.csv` is "wrong," yet §2 of the same document still states its baseline config is "Verified against `data/bots_config.csv` (champion row)" — also against `CLAUDE.md` §3 item 2 ("Do not read a config fact from it") and `data/archive/README-v1-ledger.md`'s description of that file as "the discredited hand-written record." — `ic-trailing-stop-backtest.md` banner vs. §2; also `CLAUDE.md` §3 item 2.

15. **Retired ≥15-condor go-live gate possibly still live in code.** `docs/evidence-standards.md` §5 retired the legacy ≥15-condor go-live bar ("Do not reinstate a 15-condor bar"); `docs/audit-report-2026-08-08.md` finding DA-3 and `docs/evidence-standards-redesign-proposal-2026-08-08.md` (its own S-7 finding) both assert `scripts/report.py` line 141 and `STATUS.md` line 17 still publish that exact retired gate. The `STATUS.md` instance read during this audit shows the Readiness board's G2 gate already at "≥20 clean condors," not ≥15 — so this may already be resolved and simply not reflected back into DA-3's status, or the ≥15 bar may live in a code path not visible in the rendered doc. Worth a direct check of `scripts/report.py`. — `evidence-standards.md` §5 vs. `audit-report-2026-08-08.md` DA-3 / `evidence-standards-redesign-proposal-2026-08-08.md` S-7 vs. observed `STATUS.md`.

16. **`compliance.csv` identity ambiguity.** `STATUS.md`'s Readiness-board G5 gate cites its data source as "`data/compliance.csv`," while `data/archive/README-v1-ledger.md` identifies a file of the same base name as v1 data living in `data/archive/` — "the v1 G5 compliance scores... never a reporting input." If a distinct post-cutover `data/compliance.csv` doesn't yet exist, G5 risks reading (or being confused with) the frozen v1 file the README forbids citing. — `STATUS.md` Readiness board G5 vs. `data/archive/README-v1-ledger.md`.

17. **`docs/AI Agentic.pdf` referenced in the project's own file map but does not exist** in the working copy used for this audit (exhaustive search across all upload paths found only `docs/AI Agent Stack.md`). Not a doc-vs-doc conflict, but a broken pointer worth flagging alongside the others. — `CLAUDE.md` §6 (research references) vs. observed filesystem.

18. **`AI Agent Stack.md`'s governance model vs. `CLAUDE.md` §5's amended OA authority.** The Stack doc's Architecture-B design states "the human is the sole writer of rulings; the orchestrator is the sole applier of ruled changes" and "no subagent can toggle a bot" (§6). `CLAUDE.md` §5 (amended 2026-08-04) instead has Claude executing OA edits directly. Neither document cites the other; unclear whether the Stack doc's design was ever adopted or was superseded in practice. — `docs/AI Agent Stack.md` §6 vs. `CLAUDE.md` §5.

19. **`backtest-ingest-protocol.md` cites retired/renamed files.** Its header says it "Pairs with `daily-results-process.md`... and `daily-brief-spec.md`," and Step 4 instructs "Update `current-state.md`." The project's file map names `docs/daily-loop-spec.md` instead, and `docs/history-index.md` states `docs/current-state.md` "was retired in cleanup Block 2," its live facts now in `docs/state.md`. Applying F-2 alone would reproduce the exact confound F-36 warns about; the two findings are not cross-referenced in the source. — `day0-release-2026-08-05.md` F-2 vs. F-36.
